use pursuant to the Federal Rules of Civil
Procedure, taken by notice and agreement on the
27th day of January, 2023, via 400m.

ST TPULAT £F ON S

Deposition is being taken by agreement
of counsel for the plaintiff and the defendant.

It is agreed that the deposition shali be taken in
machine shorthand by Cathy H. Kerley, Notary
Public and Court Reporter, that the signature of
the witness to the completed deposition is waived,
and that the witness may be sworn by the said
Notary Public.

Tt is further agreed that all
formalities as to caption, notice, certificate and
mode of transmission are waived, and that the
deposition is taken subject to the usual
exceptions as to irrelevancy, incompetency and
immateriality, which are reserved to the hearing
of the cause, except as to the form of the

question.

INTRODUCTION
WELCOME TO FOOD CITY

We are pleased to present you with a copy of our Food City associate
handbook. It has been designed to help you know your company better.
Whether you have been with us for a short time or for a number of years,
we want you to know we appreciate the contribution you are making
to the continued successful operation of Food City. We believe it is our
responsibility to keep you accurately informed of our company’s policies
and procedures.

As Food City grows, we want you to grow with it. Your company has made
considerable progress since its beginning, and the credit goes to each
individual associate—your job is important to our continued growth.

This handbook is a summary of the principles for which we stand, the
benefits which you may enjoy, and the obligations which you assume as
an associate. We do not intend that any “rule” or “policy” cause an undue
hardship for any associate. We set them forth simply to let you know
what you may expect from Food City and what will be expected of you,

This handbook is intended as a guide, and it is not intended to be,
nor should it be, construed as an employment contract. The employee
benefits, personnel policies, and rules contained in this handbook will
remain in effect unless changes are considered necessary.

Food City reserves the right to make changes in this handbook, as it deems
appropriate, with or without notice. This handbook supersedes any
benefits, policies, or rules previously written or communicated.

Employment is terminable at the will of either the associate or Food City
at any time, and no representative of Food City has any authority to make
any contrary agreement except in writing and signed by the CEO.

You are requested to read your handbook carefully and keep it for future
reference. If you should have any questions concerning the policies or
benefits outlined in this handbook, please ask your supervisor who will be
glad to help you.

We welcome our new associates and extend best wishes for continued
success to those who have become a part of the growth and progress of
our company. We are pleased to have you as a member of our team.

Sincerely,

lin Lebo

Steven C. Smith, President & Chief Executive Officer

THE TEN COMMANDMENTS OF
GOOD BUSINESS

“OUR BUSINESS IS PEOPLE”

CUSTOMERS are the most important people in any business.

CUSTOMERS are not dependent upon us—we are dependent upon
them.

CUSTOMERS are not an interruption of our work—they are the
purpose of it.

CUSTOMERS do us a favor when they call—we are not doing them a
favor by serving them.

CUSTOMERS are part of our business—not outsiders.

CUSTOMERS are not a cold statistic—they are flesh and blood human
beings with feelings and emotions like our own.

A CUSTOMER is not someone to argue or match wits with.

CUSTOMERS are the ones who bring us their wants—it is our job to fill
these wants.

CUSTOMERS are deserving of the most courteous and attentive
treatment we can give them.

CUSTOMERS are the lifeblood of this and every other business—they
provide our jobs!

K-VA-T
1 000004

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 122 of 199

THE CUSTOMER AND YOU

in your contacts with customers, YOU, for these moments, represent
our Company, as far as they are concerned. Our reputation with the
customer is at stake—and it’s in YOUR hands. Your actions and attitude
can strengthen the Company or can quickly turn the customer against us.
You have everything to GAIN by being courteous, friendly, and helpful, and
everything to LOSE by being discourteous, unfriendly, and unconcerned,
IF, JUST ONCE, WE FAIL TO SERVE THEM WELL—THEY MAY LEAVE US, NEVER
TO RETURN.

Here are some pointers to keep in mind in your relationship with our
“Boss,” or the CUSTOMER:

1.

LEARN TO SMILE, OFTEN. Start an.epidemic of smiling! It’s so much
easier to smile than it is to frown. Give a smile and receive one in
return.

ALWAYS GREET THE CUSTOMER. A friendly, “GOOD MORNING!” anda
sincere “THANK YOU!” makes shopping and working a pleasure.

NEVER ARGUE. Be attentive to a complaint. Remain courteous and, if
there is a misunderstanding, call the Manager.

AVOID OVER-FAMILIARITY, Call customers “Mr.,” “Ma’am,” “Miss,” “Sir,”
or “Mrs.” Be careful of teasing customers. Do not make remarks that
might be misunderstood about an item a customer has purchased.
Since it is in the shopping cart, we must assume it was needed,
wanted, and the customer can afford it.

BE HONEST. Never misrepresent a product.

REMEMBER THE FOOD CITY GUARANTEE. All merchandise is sold with
a 100% satisfaction guarantee. The full purchase price is refunded if
the merchandise has not given complete satisfaction.

GIVE EQUAL SERVICE TO ALL CUSTOMERS. Every customer is
important and is welcome in our store.

DO NOT SOLICIT TIPS BY WORD OR ACTION. Your Company
compensates you for the services you perform, Solicitation of tips is
grounds for immediate dismissal.

“IT’S THE LITTLE THINGS IN LIFE” THAT COUNT —

Say “THANK YOU” for consideration shown, and purchases made.
Say “EXCUSE ME” when you must interrupt.

K-VA-T
2 000005

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 123 of 199

Say “PLEASE” when you ask for something.

Say “I’LL FIND OUT” instead of “| DON’T KNOW” when asked a
question.

10. At Food City, we have a reputation for speedy checking, bagging,
and loading. We must all work at it and cooperate fully. We will call
to the front additional “courtesy clerks” as needed to quickly handle
customer traffic. At this point, THE CUSTOMER does not want to be
delayed. We have a duty to see that delays are kept at a minimum.
Any associate, regardless of job classification, is expected to assist on
the “front” when requested. Our customers make payday possible -
Let’s keep them happy!

EMPLOYMENT POLICIES AND PROCEDURES
OUR WORKING RELATIONSHIP

We welcome you to our Company and want you to enjoy your work here.
Our management team is pledged to help you in every way.

During your first few months with us, you will find your supervisor and
coworkers alike working more closely with you than at any time of your
employment here. Their years of experience will give you a “helping
hand” and “head start” with your orientation and job training. Food City
will always be interested in you and your job here. Should you ever wish
to leave our employment or should we desire to end the employment
relationship, we both are free to do so at will. If this should happen, we
encourage a notice of separation on your part and prior counseling on
our part, depending on circumstances, giving us both time to hopefully
prevent any misunderstanding or unfortunate separations.

Thank you for joining our team. We need your unique contribution and
look forward to a mutually beneficial work experience.

At Food City, we recognize that, other than our customers, you are our most
important asset. Without you, the Company cannot function and prosper.
No matter what your role, you are an important part of our organization.

Therefore, with respect to you and all our other associates, Food City’s
goals and objectives are:

1. To provide for all our associates a healthy and wholesome
atmosphere in which to work, not only in physical surroundings but
in attitudes, actions, and communications.

K-VACT
3 000006

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 124 of 199

2, To make sure that everyone in our organization knows what is
expected of him or her and is properly compensated for the work he
or she performs.

3. Torecognize the rights of each individual.
4. To allow plenty of opportunity for promotion and advancement.

5. To recognize that our customers are the reason for our existence as
an organization and to serve them courteously and serve them well.

6. Toconduct all our operations in an ethical and upstanding manner
in order to generate, by our actions, a reputation as a progressive,
growing Company, eager to make the community a better place in
which to live.

The Company’s employment objective is to select individuals who meet
high standards of personality, character, education, and occupational
qualifications, who can carry on our work competently, who have a capacity
for growth, and who will become an important part of our organization.

Food City realizes that its fundamental strength and future growth depend
directly upon the contribution made by each person within its organization,
that productivity and efficiency result from real job satisfaction and from
the opportunity each person receives and accepts for his or her individual
self- development.

The Company’s employment policy is, therefore, designed to:

1. Place each associate, insofar as is possible, in a position that suits his
or her natural aptitudes and skills.

2. Offer each associate opportunity for self-development and
advancement through on-the-job training,

3. Recognize the importance of the work of each associate to the overall
Company performance.

EQUAL EMPLOYMENT OPPORTUNITY

It is Food City’s policy to grant equal employment opportunities to all
qualified persons without regard to race, color, sex, religion, age, national
origin, disability, veteran’s status, or any other protected status.

K-VA-T
4 000007

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 125 of 199

LEGAL WORK STATUS

The Immigration Reform and Control Act of 1986 requires that all associates
hired by Food City provide documentation proving that they have a legal
right to work in the United States.

In compliance with this Act, all job offers extended to successful applicants
are made contingent upon the receipt of the required documentation and
completion of USCIS Form 1-9.

NO HARASSMENT

Food City does not tolerate the harassment of its associates, customers, or
vendors. Any form of harassment relating to an individual's race, color, sex
(including same sex), religion, age, national origin, handicap or disability,
citizenship status, veteran status, or any other protected activity or
category, is a violation of this policy and will result in disciplinary action
up to and including dismissal.

Food City will not allow anyone to create an intimidating, hostile, or
offensive environment. This includes supervisors, associates, or non-
associates such as vendors.

The term “harassment” as used in this policy includes, but is not limited
to, the following:

* Offensive remarks, comments, jokes, slurs, or verbal conduct
pertaining to an individual’s race, color, sex, religion, age, national
origin, handicap or disability, citizenship status, or any other
protected category

- Offensive pictures, drawings, photographs, or other graphic conduct
or communications, including e-mail, faxes, and copies pertaining to
an individual's race, color, sex, religion, age, national origin, handicap
or disability, citizenship status, or any other protected category

* Offensive sexual remarks, sexual advances, or requests for sexual
favors, regardless of the gender of the individuals involved

» Offensive physical conduct, including touching, regardless of the
gender of the individuals involved

- Retaliation against an employee for refusing to respond to requests
for sexual favors, for reporting a violation of this policy, or for
participating in an investigation conducted under this policy

If you feel you are the victim of harassment, please notify anyone in
management. You do not necessarily have to first notify your immediate
supervisor of such a situation. Feel free to contact anyone inthe Company’s
K-VA-T
5 000008

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 126 of 199

management, including the Vice President of Human Resources, the
Company President, or the Chairman of the Board. You are responsible for
bringing your concerns about possible policy violations to our attention so
that we can take appropriate actions to address your concerns,

HOW AND WHY YOU WERE SELECTED

The success of Food City and your future here depends upon you. This
is why we carefully select our associates through application, careful
interview, and reference checking.

PRE-EMPLOYMENT SCREENINGS AND EXAMS

After an offer of employment has been given, but before you actually start
working, you may be asked to take a health examination at the Company’s
expense. Drug and alcohol screening may also be required. You may also
be required to take another physical examination or drug and alcohol
screening, at Company expense, when Food City determines it is job-
related and consistent with business necessity.

ILL ASSOCIATES

Food City recognizes that associates with life-threatening illnesses,
including but not limited to cancer, heart disease, AIDS, or other
disabilities, may wish to continue in as many of their normal pursuits as
their condition allows, including work. As long as these associates are
able to meet performance standards, with or without accommodation(s),
and medical evidence indicates that their condition is not a danger to
themselves, others, or property, Food City will endeavor to be sensitive to
their conditions and treat them consistently with other ill associates. At the
same time, the Company must provide a safe workplace for all associates
and customers.

Food City offers the following range of resources available through Human
Resources:

» Management and associate education and information on illness or
disabilities including specific life-threatening illnesses

+ Referral to agencies and organizations that offer supportive services
for life-threatening illnesses or disabilities

* Benefit consultation to assist associates in effectively managing
health, leave, and other benefits

K-VA-T
G 000009

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 127 of 199

TYPES OF EMPLOYMENT
There are four (4) personnel classifications:

FULL-TIME — Any person who is regularly scheduled to work thirty-five
(35) hours per week or more is classified as a full-time associate and is
eligible to receive certain benefits.

PART-TIME — Any person who is regularly scheduled to work less than
thirty-five (35) hours per week or whose work schedule must be specifically
arranged because of other commitments of the associate (such as school)
is classified as a part-time associate.

PART-TIME STUDENT TRAINEE — A part-time associate who is a full-time
student of an accredited school. When school is in session, twenty (20)
hours is the maximum time a part-time student trainee can work in one
(1) week, except when a full-day, school holiday occurs on a day the store
is open. The twenty (20) hours weekly limitation maybe increased by eight
(8) hours for each holiday. When school is not in session, they cannot work
more than eight (8) hours per day and forty (40) hours per week. These
associates will be paid student rates,

TEMPORARY — Any person who is hired for temporary work on any
schedule which does not exceed four (4) calendar months. Temporary
personnel may be ona part-time or full-time schedule of work hours.

NEW HIRES

[tis the policy of Food City to hire all new entry-level associates {other than
temporaries) as part-time.

EMPLOYMENT OF RELATIVES

Family members and close relatives are permitted to work in the same
store or work location. Close relatives are not permitted to work directly
for, or in a supervisory role of, a relative. “Close relatives” defined as
spouse, in-laws, brothers, sisters, children, or parents. Any exceptions to
this policy must be approved by the Vice-President of Human Resources
and is only granted in unusual circumstances.

ORGANIZATION MEMBERSHIP IS NOT REQUIRED

Our federal law gives you the right not to have to join any organization in
order to hold your job. It is not necessary for any associate to have to join

K-VA-T
7 900010

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 128 of 199

and pay dues and other union fees to any union in order to work at Food
City.

It is the policy of our Company to pay wages that compare favorably with
other wages in similar businesses in our area—to give the best possible
working conditions we can to treat each associate as an individual.

Even more important, it is our intention to do everything possible to fu rnish
excellent job security for anyone who works in our Company and provide
that person with the best possible job satisfaction. It is our Company policy
to promote from within the Company whenever possible, Only when a
person with the qualifications for the job to be filled is not available, do we
look to outside sources.

We believe that a labor union is an unnecessary third party and could
seriously interfere with our ability to treat you and our customer in the
manner you both deserve.

In the best interests of you and the Company, we will oppose, by every
moral and legal means, any attempt by any outside organization to disturb
or break up the fine relationship we enjoy.

WE WANT YOU TO FEEL AT HOME

You may feel a little strange in your new surroundings. However, it will not
take you long to get acquainted. Your fellow associates, especially your
supervisor or Store Manager, will work closely with you and will provide
you with all the information you need to better understand our policies
and practices. If you need help, ask them.

OUR PERFORMANCE REVIEW —
YOUR PROGRESS REPORT

All of us like to know how we are doing on the job. Day-to-day comments
from department managers help, but now and then there Is a need to

evaluate the performance of every person under their supervision. The
purpose of these reviews is to provide management and you with a periodic
review of your performance on the job and to identify any areas where you
can improve your performance. Your review will cover the following points:

1, The amount of work you accomplish

2. The accuracy and thoroughness of your work

PROMOTIONS

At Food City, as in all organizations, different positions have different
degrees of difficulty and relative importance to the operation of the
Company. The more important of these positions are designated as
full-time. Certain other positions may be filled by either full-time or
part-time associates as required by the demands of the particular store
or department. Within these full-time or part-time positions there are
varying degrees of responsibility, proficiency, and/or ability which are all
considered in the placement of a person in a particular job.

From time to time, vacancies occur for whatever reason, and at this time a
replacement must be selected.

In making a selection to fill a vacancy, it is our policy to always look first at
the junior associates within the Company for qualified candidates. Here,
job performance, ability to learn, and aptitude are most important. Should
we find more than one candidate of equal qualifications, the one with the
K-VA-T
9 000012

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 130 of 199

longest length of service would be selected. Only when no junior associate
is found qualified, do we go out of the Company to hire.

GUIDELINES FOR LAYOFF AND RECALL

Food City has a history of full employment, and we do not expect to have
layoffs. However, if economic or business conditions create an unusual
situation which makes it necessary to cut back our work force, then
each job will be carefully checked along with each associate’s record,
Qualifications, past performance, and length of service of each associate
will be the guidelines upon which such decisions are made.

IMPORTANCE OF YOUR LENGTH OF SERVICE—
SENIORITY

At Food City, we believe in recognizing your seniority and length of service.
Seniority is given to associates through length of service which brings
certain privileges and considerations, We have a good seniority program.
Usually, this date is the day you were hired for the first time. However,
if your seniority has been broken through resignation, discharge, or
extended layoff, then your seniority date becomes the last day you were
hired following the break in seniority. Associates having the same date of
hire will hold seniority in alphabetical order.

Your seniority is important to you in the following ways:

1. Itrepresents the investment in time and money by Food City in your
training and experience. This gives you more job opportunity and
promotion possibilities as your experience increases along with your
seniority.

2, Your seniority has direct relationship to certain of our important
associate benefits which are based upon length of service, such as
paid vacations.

3. Incase we should ever have a layoff, due to business conditions
beyond our control, your seniority will give you more work
opportunity provided you have necessary qualifications and
seniority.

4. Your seniority is also recognized through your length-of-service
awards.

IS SOMETHING TROUBLING YOU?

When a group of people are closely working together, a few
misunderstandings are bound to occur. We all recognize that everything
is not going to run smoothly all the time. If something is bothering you, if
you are discouraged, worried, or upset about your work or some outside
problem, you cannot work at full efficiency. Feel free to discuss any problem
with your Department Manager or Store Manager. They are interested in
your welfare and will do what they can to help the situation. If neither can
help, you will be referred to someone who can.

SPEAKING UP—RESOLVING COMPLAINTS
OR PROBLEMS THROUGH HEARINGS

In the event the efforts of your supervisor or Store Manager to resolve a
problem for you fail, and if you feel itis of such nature that top management
of the Company can help you solve it, then please communicate, preferably
in writing, with the Company Vice President of Human Resources in
Abingdon, VA.

Food City cares about all Company associates, and we are anxious to hear
about any problems or complaints that cannot be resolved at the store
level. There will be absolutely no discrimination against anyone who
appeals a problem, so please, don’t let the problem grow. Write, or if you
feel you cannot adequately write, then call the Vice President of Human
Resources immediately. You will receive a hearing by the Vice President of
Human Resources,

Should you still be dissatisfied, you may make one last appeal to the Chief
Executive Officer, who will render the final and binding decision.

The purpose of our hearing procedure is to help you work out any

PROGRESSIVE DISCIPLINE POLICY

At Food City, we believe discipline is not meant to be punishment in
any way. However, when it becomes necessary to change an associate’s
performance or behavior from unacceptable to acceptable, our Progressive
Discipline Policy enables us to endeavor to do so in a consistent manner.

Normal steps in the disciplinary process are outlined below. However,
at management’s discretion, management may enter into any level of
disciplinary action, including termination.

Oral Correction—The supervisor will provide an oral correction to the
associate, A written record of this correction will be placed in the associate’s
personnel file.

Written Correction—The supervisor will consult with higher management
and prepare a written correction. The associate will be asked to sign
indicating receipt of a copy of the written correction, and a copy will be
placed in the associate’s personnel file,

Suspension Without Pay—If the written correction does not correct the
problem, the associate may be suspended without pay for a minimum of
three (3) days. This suspension without pay must have the approval of the
Chief Executive Officer.

Termination—The supervisor may recommend termination of associates.
Recommendations will be reviewed by management, which will determine
the action to be taken,

If the associate feels the termination is unfair, he or she may use our
Open Door Policy or Hearing Procedure, if appropriate. Based upon this
discussion, the appropriate Company officer will make a final determination
in the case.

Management reserves the right to enter into any level of disciplinary action
or termination depending on the circumstances.

YOUR GUIDE TO PERSONAL CONDUCT

Food City wants to provide a good work environment for all associates. This
desire is expressed in many forms: safe working conditions, maintenance
of facilities and equipment, equitable wage structures, and progressive
K-VA-T
13 000016

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 134 of 199

benefit programs. In turn, it is reasonable to expect a good productive
effort and the recognition of responsibility on the part of associates.

Each of us has the responsibility to our fellow workers to conduct ourseives
according to certain rules of good behavior or conduct. In any business,
some rules are needed to help everyone work together by letting them
know what they can and cannot do. We expect our associates to follow
our Company rules and show good behavior and efficiency. For these
reasons, we have included, in our handbook, a number of work rules. You
are expected to read, understand and follow these rules in your day-to-day
work.

Disciplinary action, whether oral or written, is given only for the purpose
of correcting someone for doing something wrong. Having to dismiss an
associate is unpleasant for everyone, and we try to work with each associate
to avoid such action. However, failure to follow our work rules is against
the best interests of our other associates and our Company, and usually
leads to dismissal. Should you have any questions, at ali, concerning any
work rule listed, please see your supervisor.

In order to be absolutely fair, we have what we call a “wash-out” period for
certain disciplinary actions. An associate should not be penalized unless
there is a repetitious problem. Therefore, after having made a “mistake,”
we will “wipe the slate clean” if no more rule infractions occur within
twelve (12} months from the date of the last rule violation.

Food City rules are basically common sense, requiring conduct acceptable
to a customer service oriented employment environment. When these
common sense practices are violated, disciplinary action may be taken.
While the following list is not all-inclusive, some of the violations which
can result in disciplinary action—including discharge—are;

TERMINATION OF EMPLOYMENT

Termination of employment is always unpleasant and costly so you can
be sure it won’t be considered lightly. If, however, discharge becomes
necessary, advance notice may or may not be given depending on the
circumstances, and unused vacation will not be paid.

OTHER EMPLOYMENT

We realize that many of our associates, and particularly our part-timers,
have other employment in one form or another. This is agreeable to us so
long as it neither interferes with the associate’s work attendance or job
performance nor creates a conflict of interest,

The following are considered by our Company as a “Conflict of Interest.”

1. Active ownership of, or platonic interest in, a business operation
competitive with ours in any way.

2. Employment with a competitor doing business in the retail grocery
industry.

3, Employment directly or indirectly with a sales company (direct
vendor, manufacturer, or food industry brokerage) which makes sales
and/or service calls on any store owned by our Company or those
owned by our competition.

Any associate whose other employment is considered a “Conflict of
Interest” will be subject to immediate dismissal.

SHOULD YOU RESIGN

We hope you remain with us fora long time. However, sometimes personal
affairs force a change in jobs. In such cases, please give your supervisor at
least two weeks’ notice. This courtesy will allow your supervisor enough
time to adjust working schedules and secure a replacement. Also, your
advance notice will reflect favorably on your employment record. Payroll
checks will be made available on the next regularly scheduled payday.

Whenever possible, an exit interview will be conducted for anyone who
resigns. It is to your advantage to participate in this interview so that we
can give you information about your benefits. Also, we will want to have
your honest opinion on our Company policies and practices which will
enable us to improve our relationship with everyone.

COMPANY REFERENCES

We are glad to cooperate with former associates by answering, as fully
as possible, any inquiries which may be received from their prospective
employers. Your Company, however, does not give general open “To Whom
It May Concern” letters of recommendation. Company references may not
be issued by anyone other than the Human Resources Department.

RE-EMPLOYMENT

Anyone who has voluntarily left our employment and whose work
performance met our standards while employed will be considered for re-
employment status in any position for which they are suitable, and possess
appropriate skills and experience. Such individuals will be considered
as new associates, with no continuation of any previously earned but
forfeited Company benefits or seniority. These associates will complete all
pre-employment paperwork,

OUR PAY POLICY

It is our Company policy to pay wages and salaries comparable to those
paid for similar jobs and services in our surrounding area. We maintain our
wage and salary structure by periodically conducting wage surveys. We
adjust our wage and salary ranges in accordance with the business and
general economic conditions of our Company.

The pay range for your individual job classification is determined by the
requirements of your job with such factors considered as responsibility,
skill, training, education, and working conditions. Wage increases are
based upon your record of performance on the job and your particular job
classification.

PAYDAY

Paydays at Food City are handled on a weekly basis. Your paycheck for
the previous workweek will be available to you every Thursday, The pay
you receive is for work you performed during the previous workweek.
Paychecks are typically deposited directly to your bank or pay card that
you have indicated. If you have elected to receive pay through a paper
check, you may pick it up on Thursday.

SHOULD YOU FIND AN ERROR IN YOUR PAY

Every precaution is taken to avoid errors in your paycheck. If an error does
occur, tell your Manager, If an error is found, the adjustment will be made
on the next paycheck,

OVERTIME

For hourly associates, all work performed in excess of forty (40) hours in
a payroll week will be paid at one-and-one-half (1-1/2) times the average
hourly rate of pay earned during the first forty (40) hours.

PREMIUM RATE

Full-time, retail associates who are scheduled and work between 9:00 P.M.
and 6:00 A.M. receive a 50 cent-per-hour premium for all hours worked
between these times. Additionally, those full-time associates working on
Sunday will also receive the 50 cent-per-hour premium. However, premium
rate is paid only during the first forty (40) hours worked during any week.

REST PERIODS

Rest periods are paid time provided by the Company for your relaxation
and enjoyment. Rest periods are given for not more than fifteen (15)
minutes in duration during every four (4) hours or more worked. All rest
periods are to be recorded as directed by your Store Manager.

Rest periods are scheduled by your Department Manager when they will
least interfere with the operation of the store or service to the customers.

Normally, you are not to leave the store premises during rest periods.
Should you need to leave for any reason, please get the prior approval of
your Store Manager.

19 000022

MEAL PERIODS

Anyone working more than six (6) hours per day
is normally scheduled to take one (1) hour for a
meal. This meal period will be scheduled by your
supervisor and will be around the middle of your
shift. You must never exceed five (5) hours working

period. For night stock associates only, the meal

free to use it as you see fit. Please try to make your personal calls during
this time. Always take the full time set aside for your meal period unless
requested to do otherwise by your Manager. During this meal period, you
are not to perform any work whatsoever. It’s important to record your time
at the beginning and end of your meal period.

YOUR PAYROLL DEDUCTIONS

Does your paycheck represent the full amount of your earnings? No. We are
required to deduct your federal and, where applicable, state income tax
from your paycheck. This deducted amount is turned over to the United
States and State treasuries, and you are given credit for it on your income
tax at the end of the year. The amount of the tax deduction is determined
by your earnings and the number of your dependents. Each year, you will
receive a W-2 showing your total earnings for the year and the amount of
taxes withheld.

Also, out of your paycheck will come deductions for Social Security at the
rate established by law. Your Company contributes an equal amount of
that deducted from your wages. Any other deductions such as health plan
coverage, etc., must be authorized by you before they can be made,

represent real doilars to you and your family. These benefits include such
items as paid vacations, paid holidays, various types of insurances, etc. We

YOUR VACATION WITH PAY

We believe that our full-time associates should have
a time of rest and relaxation each and every year.
Vacation with pay is for this purpose and is one of
the ways we show our appreciation to you for your
length of service and good work. For this reason,
it is Company policy to require that at least one (1)
week of earned vacation be taken each year. Vacation
time due in excess of one (1) week may be taken in
increments of one day or more ata time.

Full-time associates who have been with the Company for a minimum of
twelve (12) continuous months since their last date of hire or the date on
which they became a full-time associate, whichever is later, will receive a
vacation according to the following schedule:

LENGTH OF SERVICE PAID VACATION
After 1 year of continuous service 1 week

After 3 years of continuous service 2 weeks

After 10 years of continuous service 3 weeks

After 20 years of continuous service 4 weeks

Continuous service of one (1) year means twelve (12) continuous
months since the last date of employment or since becoming a full-time
associate, In most instances, the date you become a full-time associate
is established as your vacation effective date. In the event an associate
is off on a leave of absence, the vacation effective date will be adjusted
accordingly. An example would be an associate who worked for ten (10)
months, was off for two (2) months on a personal leave of absence, and
returned. That associate would have to work two (2) additional months in
order to be eligible for a regular vacation period, i.e., the ten (10) months
previous uninterrupted service and then the two (2) months when he or
she returned from their leave would make the equivalent of twelve (12)
continuous months.

Vacations may be taken at any time during the year, except for the heavy
holiday period. We must consider that we are a service industry and that
our customers are our real employers, and as such, their demands must
be met first, vacations scheduled second. In order to be sure we maintain
sufficient manpower to serve our customers, all vacations to be taken

K-VA-T
21 000024

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 142 of 199

during the peak vacation period of May 15 - September 15 will be scheduled
no later than April 15. In order to do this, each Store Manager will have
each department head draw up a schedule reflecting your requested
period. This will be accomplished by allowing the more senior associates
first choice and most junior associates last choice.

Vacations taken outside this peak period may be requested on shorter
notice, but it is always best to allow at least a thirty (30)-day planning time.

Due to variation in the number of hours worked by hourly associates
during the year, and in order to be fair to all concerned, vacation pay will
be the total of regular and premium hours for the fifty-two (52) calendar
weeks prior to your full- time date (vacation effective date) divided by fifty-
two (52), then multiplied by your current straight-time rate of pay. For this
vacation hour's calculation, vacation taken as time off will be included as
time worked.

May we repeat again —- vacation is earned by completing a full year of
continuous service; vacation is never prorated but rather earned totally by
passing the vacation effective date. You will become eligible for vacation
pay only after passing your vacation effective date.

Part-time service will not be counted in determining length of service.

An associate who voluntarily quits without giving the Company at least
two (2) weeks’ notice, or is discharged, forfeits all vacation rights.

HOLIDAYS

Full-time associates, after the successful completion of ninety (90) calendar
days, shall be eligible for the following holidays:

*
*
e
*
*.
+
.

New Year's Day
Memorial Day
Independence Day
Your Birthday
Labor Day
Thanksgiving Day
Christmas Day

To qualify for holiday pay, you must work the scheduled day before and
the scheduled day after a holiday, unless your absence on either of these
days has been excused in writing by your Store Manager. Holiday pay will
amount to the average number of hours worked on a daily basis during the
prior thirteen (13) weeks. This average will be calculated by adding up the
total hours worked at regular or premium pay during the prior thirteen (13)
weeks divided by thirteen (13) then further divided by five (5) to determine

K-VA-T
22 000025

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 143 of 199

the length of the average work day, then multiplied by the current rate of
pay at straight time. For this purpose, vacation time taken as time off will
be included as time worked. For example: if your average workweek in the
prior thirteen (13) weeks is forty (40) hours, we would divide by five (5) to
determine that your average work day is eight (8) hours, Then your holiday
pay would be eight (8) hours multiplied by your straight-time rate of pay.

Holiday hours are not included in computing overtime for the week in
which the overtime occurs. Associates will receive holiday pay no matter
what day in the week the holiday falls. Should you be required to work on
a holiday, you shall be paid straight-time hourly rate for the time worked in
addition to your holiday pay. If you are scheduled to work on a holiday and
you then fail to show up and perform the work, you will not receive holiday
pay unless you have an acceptable excuse for failing to report to work.

If the paid holiday falls during your vacation, you will be paid for the holiday
in addition to your vacation, but an additional day off will not be granted.
To be eligible for a paid holiday that falls within your vacation period, you
must have worked your last regularly scheduled workday in the week prior
to your vacation and your first regularly scheduled workday in the week
after your vacation period,

Associates on any type of leave of absence other than two weeks’ military
reserve training leave, will not receive pay for holidays during their leave.

It is Company policy to require time off be taken for your birthday holiday.
Whenever it is notin the best interests of the store for your actual birthday
to be taken as a holiday, another day close to the holiday, and mutually
agreeable to both you and your Store Manager, shall be substituted.

BEREAVEMENT PAY

Allactively employed full-time associates who have completed their ninety
(90)-day waiting period may receive scheduled time off with pay if they
attend the funeral of a member of their immediate family. The immediate
family is defined as father, mother, grandparents, grandchildren, husband,
wife, child, sister, brother, and immediate step-relatives. The associate may
receive a maximum of three (3) scheduled work days off with pay starting
the day of the funeral and two days prior. Bereavement pay is based on the
average number of hours worked on a daily basis during the prior thirteen
(13) weeks, less any hours worked.

This bereavement pay is intended to offset lost income to you during this
sad period. Should you suffer no loss of income due to a change of schedule
still allowing you to work your regular schedule during this pay period, you
would have no loss of income.

For associates in their waiting period, and part-time or temporary
associates, funeral teave without pay will be granted upon the death ofa
member of the immediate family.

PERSONAL LEAVE OF ABSENCE

An unpaid leave of absence may be granted for personal reasons to any
associate after completion of their ninety (90)-day waiting period. All leave
of absence requests must be presented in writing to the Store Manager,
who shall forward it, along with his recommendation, to the Vice President
of Human Resources for consideration. The appropriate officers of the
Company shail then decide, within a reasonable time, whether or not to
grant the request and advise In writing of the decision.

All such leaves of absence shall be for a specific period of time, not to
exceed ninety (90) days, and must be requested two (2) weeks in advance
of the starting date, whenever possible. The Company may, if requested,
extend a leave of absence for a period up to an additional ninety (90) days.

K-VA-T
28 000031
Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 149 of 199

All such approved leaves of absence will contain a specific date to be
available to return to work. The associate on leave of absence, who desires
to return, must reapply at least two (2) weeks prior to this scheduled
date. There is no guarantee of immediate re-employment; however, the
associate will be considered for re-employment when a suitable position
becomes available, and a new date to report is set. Any associate who
fails to return to work on this date will be considered to have quit without
notice, Any associate, while on such a leave of absence, who accepts other
employment will be considered to have quit.

An associate does not earn any benefits during a personal leave of absence.
Examples would be vacation, holiday pay, etc.

Further, during an unpaid leave, the Company does not continue to pay
for the health plan coverage the Company provides the associate, The
associate should contact the Store Manager to make arrangements for
payment prior to the leave starting date if he or she desires to maintain the
insurance program during the leave of absence.

FAMILY AND MEDICAL LEAVE OF ABSENCE

Your Company has always been concerned about any associate’s need
for a leave of absence. Our policies have always been generous, in that
respect, and designed to meet your needs, On August 5, 1993, a new
federal law became effective which governs many leave of absence
situations for practically all of your Company’s full-time associates and
for many part-time associates. This law is called the Family and Medical
Leave Act of 1993 (FMLA). A brief summary of associate’s rights is printed
below in the language suggested by the U.S. Department of Labor. All of
your Company’s leave of absence policies pertaining to family and medical
leaves will be administered in compliance with the FMLA. In the event that
there is a conflict between any leave policy and the requirements of the
law, the law will prevail. In some instances, your Company’s policies may
be more generous than that required by law.

Eligible employees may take up to twelve (12) weeks of unpaid, job-
protected leave each year for specified family and medical reasons.

Employee Eligibility: To be eligible for family or medical leave, employees
must have worked at least:

1. Twelve (12) months for the Company; and
2. 1,250 hours for the Company over the previous twelve (12) months.

Conditions Triggering Leave: Family and medical leave must involve one
or more of the following reasons:

K-VA-T
29 000032

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37 _ Filed 04/11/23. Page 150 of 199

1. Tocare for a newly born child, or placement of a child with the
employee for adoption or foster care.

2, Tocare for an immediate family member (spouse, child, or
employee’s parent) with a serious health condition.

3. The employee has a serious health condition which makes the
employee unable to perform the employee's job duties.

4, Because of any qualifying exigency arising out of the fact that the
employee’s spouse, son, daughter, or parent is a covered service
member on active duty, or who has been called to active duty.

5. Tocare fora covered service member with a serious injury or illness if
the employee is the spouse, child, parent, or next of kin of the service
member,

Duration Of Leave: For leave other than to care for a covered service
member, eligible employees may receive up to twelve (12) workweeks of
unpaid leave during any “rolling” twelve (12)-month period, measured
backward from the date of any family or medical leave. Family and medical
leave involving the birth or placement of a child for adoption or foster care
must be concluded within twelve (12) months of the birth or placement.

Eligible employees may take family and medical leave intermittently —
which means taking leave in blocks of time, or by reducing your normal
weekly or daily work schedule — whenever it is medically necessary to
care for a seriously ill family member, or because you are seriously ill and
unable to work, or for leave based on a qualifying exigency, or to care for a
covered service member. Intermittent leave is not permitted for birth of a
child or placement of a child for adoption or foster care.

Eligible employees may take up to twenty-six (26) weeks of leave to care
for a covered service member with a serious injury or illness in a single
twelve (12)-month period, measured forward from the date that such
leave begins.

Subject to certain conditions, eligible employees may choose (or the
Company may require you) to use accrued paid leave (such as sick time
or vacation leave) as a substitute for some or all of the family and medical
leave,

Particular limitations apply to certain leaves when both spouses are
employed by the same employer. If these limitations apply, the Company
will inform you when responding to your request for leave.

Maintenance Of Health Benefits: The Company will maintain coverage
for eligible employees and dependents (if applicable) during family and

K-VA-T
30 000033

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 151 of 199

medical leave. This coverage will be provided if you or your family were
covered under the plan before the leave was taken and on the same terms
as if you had continued to work. Where appropriate, you must make
arrangements to continue to pay your share of health plan premiums
while on leave.

In some instances, the Company may recover premiums it paid to maintain
health coverage for you and your family if you do not return to work from
your leave.

Job Restoration: Upon returning from a family and medical leave,
eligible employees will normally be restored to their original job, or to
an equivalent job with equivalent pay, benefits, and other employment
terms and conditions. However, an employee does not continue to accrue
additional benefits such as personal leave and vacation, for example, while
on family and medical leave.

Use of family and medical leave cannot resultin the loss of any employment
benefit that employees earned, or were entitled to, before using family and
medical leave.

Notice And Medical Certification: When seeking family and medical
leave, employees may be required to provide: -

1. Thirty (30) days advance notice of the need to take family and
medical leave, if the need is foreseeable.

>. Medical certifications supporting the need for leave due to a serious
health condition affecting you or an immediate family member.
Second or third medical opinions (at the Company’s expense), and
periodic recertification may also be required,

3. Periodic reports during the leave regarding your status and intent to
return to work.

4. Medical certification of fitness for duty before returning to work, if
the leave was due to your health condition.

When leave is needed to care for an immediate family member or for
the employee’s own illness, and is for planned medical treatment, the
employee must try to schedule treatment so that it will not unduly disrupt
the Company’s operation,

Extended Medical Leave: Eligible employees who have exhausted their
family and medical leave, and all other employees, may be allowed to
take extended medical leave of absence, not to exceed twelve (12) months
following the last day worked, Employees who take such extended
medical leave are not guaranteed to be returned to work or reinstatement
K-VA-T
31 000034

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 152 of 199

to a particular job, rate of pay, or shift, at the end of their extended
medical leave. However, the Company will attempt to return employees
to their regular position if it is available. If it is not available at the time
reinstatement is sought, we will attempt to place you in a similar job for
which you are qualified, if such job is available. Employees on extended
medical leave may maintain their insurance benefits, subject to policy
terms and conditions by paying the applicable COBRA premiums in a
timely manner. Employees on extended medical leave do not accrue any
additional employee benefits such as personal leave or vacation while on
extended medical leave,

Other Employment: Outside employment during your leave period
is prohibited and may result in disciplinary action, up to and including,
immediate termination of employment.

Failure to Return from Leave or Comply with this Policy: Employees
may be subject to immediate termination for:

1. Failure to return to work within twelve (12) months of the beginning
of a medical leave;

2. Failure to return to work within twelve (12) weeks of the beginning of
a family ieave;

3. Failure to return to work as scheduled following the end of a medical
or family leave;

4, Providing false or misleading information or omitting certain
information in connection with a family or medical leave; or

5. Violation of any of the Company’s rules and regulations relating
to a family or medical leave (or any other policy or performance
standard),

EMPLOYEE RIGHTS AND RESPONSIBILITIES
UNDER THE FAMILY AND MEDICAL LEAVE ACT

Basic Leave Entitlement

FMLA requires covered employers to provide up to twelve (12) weeks of
unpaid, job-protected leave to eligible employees for the following reasons:

« For incapacity due to pregnancy, prenatal medical care or child birth;

« Tocare for the employee's child after birth, or placement for adoption
or foster care;

« Tocare for the employee’s spouse, son or daughter, or parent, who
has a serious health condition; or

K-VA-T
32 000035
Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 153 of 199

« Foraserious health condition that makes the employee unable to
perform the employee's job,

Military Family Leave Entitlements

Eligible employees whose spouse, son, daughter, or parent is on covered
active duty or call to covered active duty status may use their twelve (12)-
week leave entitlement to address certain qualifying exigencies. Qualifying
exigencies may include attending certain military events, arranging for
alternative childcare, addressing certain financial and legal arrangements,
attending certain counseling sessions, and attending post-deployment
reintegration briefings.

FMLA also includes a special leave entitlement that permits eligible
employees to take up to twenty-six (26) weeks of leave to care for a covered
service member during a single twelve (12})-month period. A covered
service member is: (1) a current member of the Armed Forces, including
a member of the National Guard or Reserves, who is undergoing medical
treatment, recuperation or therapy, is otherwise in outpatient status, or
is otherwise on the temporary disability retired list, for a serious injury or
illness;* or (2) a veteran who was discharged or released under conditions
other than dishonorable at any time during the five (5)-year period prior
to the first date the eligible employee takes FMLA leave to care for the
covered veteran, and who is undergoing medical treatment, recuperation,
or therapy for a serious injury or illness."

*The FMLA definitions of “serious injury or illness” for current service
members and veterans are distinct from the FMLA definition of “serious
health condition.”

Benefits and Protections

During FMLA leave, the employer must maintain the employee's health
coverage under any “group health plan” on the same terms as if the
employee had continued to work. Upon return from FMLA leave, most
employees must be restored to their original or equivalent positions with
equivalent pay, benefits, and other employment terms.

Use of FMLA leave cannot result in the loss of any employment benefit that
accrued prior to the start of an employee’s leave.

Eligibility Requirements

Employees are eligible if they have worked for a covered employer for
at least twelve (12) months, have 1,250 hours of service in the previous
twelve (12) months, and if at least fifty (50) employees are employed by the
employer within seventy-five (75) miles.
K-VA-T
33 000036
Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 154 of 199

Definition of Serious Health Condition

A serious health condition is an illness, injury, impairment, or physical or
mental condition that involves either an overnight stay in a medical care
facility, or continuing treatment by a health care provider for a condition
that either prevents the employee from performing the functions of the
employee’s job, or prevents the qualified family member from participating
in school or other daily activities.

Subject to certain conditions, the continuing treatment requirement
may be met by a period of incapacity of more than three (3) consecutive
calendar days combined with at least two (2) visits to a health care provider
or one (1) visit and a regimen of continuing treatment, or incapacity due to
pregnancy, or incapacity due to a chronic condition. Other conditions may
meet the definition of continuing treatment.

Use of Leave

An employee does not need to use this leave entitlement in one block.
Leave can be taken intermittently or on a reduced leave schedule when
medically necessary. Employees must make reasonable efforts to
schedule leave for planned medical treatment so as not to unduly disrupt
the employer’s operations, Leave due to qualifying exigencies may also be
taken on an intermittent basis.

Substitution of Paid Leave for Unpaid Leave

Employees may choose or employers may require use of accrued paid
leave while taking FMLA leave. In order to use paid leave for FMLA leave,
employees must comply with the employer's norma! paid leave policies,

Employee Responsibilities

Employees must provide thirty (30) days’ advance notice of the need to
take EMLA leave when the need is foreseeable. When thirty (30)-days notice
is not possible, the employee must provide notice as soon as practicable
and generally must comply with an employer's normal call-in procedures.

Employees must provide sufficient information for the employer to
determine if the leave may qualify for FMLA protection and the anticipated
timing and duration of the leave. Sufficient information may include that
the employee is unable to perform job functions, the family member is
unable to perform daily activities, the need for hospitalization or continuing
treatment by a health care provider, or circumstances supporting the need
for military family leave. Employees also must inform the employer if the
requested leave is for a reason for which FMLA leave was previously taken

K-VA-T

34 000037

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 155 of 199

or certified. Employees also may be required to provide a certification and
periodic recertification supporting the need for leave.

Employer Responsibilities

Covered employers must inform employees requesting leave whether they
are eligible under FMLA. If they are, the notice must specify any additional
information required as well as the employees’ rights and responsibilities.
If they are not eligible, the employer must provide a reason for the
ineligibility.

Covered employers must inform employees if leave will be designated as
FMLA-protected and the amount of leave counted against the employee’s
leave entitlement. If the employer determines that the leave is not FMLA-
protected, the employer must notify the employee.

Unlawful Acts by Employers
FMLA makes it unlawful for any employer to:

¢ Interfere with, restrain, or deny the exercise of any right provided
under FMLA; and

* Discharge or discriminate against any person for opposing any
practice made unlawful by FMLA or for involvement in any proceeding
under or relating to FMLA.

Enforcement

An employee may file a complaint with the U.S. Department of Labor or
may bring a private lawsuit against an employer.

FMLA does not affect any Federal or State law prohibiting discrimination,
or supersede any State or local law or collective bargaining agreement
which provides greater family or medical leave rights.

FMLA section 109 (29 U.S.C. § 2619) requires FMLA covered employers to
post the text of this notice. Regulation 29 C.F.R. § 825.300(a) may require
additional disclosures. For additional information:

1-866-4US-WAGE (1-866-487-9243)

TTY: 1-877-889-5627

www.wagehour.dol. gov

U.S. Department of Labor Wage and Hour Division

PARENTAL LEAVE POLICY

The Parental Leave Policy applies to some Tennessee employees as defined
below:

A. General

Employees who have been employed for at least twelve (12) consecutive
months as full-time employees may be absent for a period not to exceed
four (4) months for adoption, pregnancy, childbirth and nursing an infant.
With regard to adoption, the four (4)-month period shall begin at the time
an employee receives custody of the child. Any time off under this policy
will also count toward an employee’s use of FMLA leave (if the employee
qualifies for FMLA leave), and such leaves shall run concurrently to the
extent possible,

B. Notice to the Company
Employees who give at least three (3) months advance notice of their

anticipated date of departure for such leave, their length of leave, and their
intention to return to full-time employment after leave, shall be restored
to their previous or similar positions with the same status, pay, length of
service credit and seniority, wherever applicable, as of the date of their
leave.

Employees who are prevented from giving three (3) months advance
notice because of a medical emergency which necessitates that leave
begin earlier than originally anticipated shall not forfeit their rights and
benefits under this section solely because of their failure to give three (3)
months advance notice.

Employees who are prevented from giving three (3) months advance notice
because the notice of adoption was received less than three (3) months in
advance shall not forfeit their rights and benefits under this section solely
because of their failure to give three (3) months advance notice.

C. Other Provisions

1. Leave will be without pay, except that employees will be required to
use accrued vacation and sick leave as under the Company’s FMLA
policy, Such leave shall not affect the employees’ right to receive
vacation time, advancement, seniority, length of service credit,
benefits, plans or programs for which the employees were eligible
at the date of their leave, and any other benefits or rights of their
employment incident to the employees’ employment position;
provided, that the Company need not provide for the cost of any
benefits, plans or programs during the period of such leave, unless
the Company so provides for all employees on leaves of absence.

2. {fan employee’s job position is so unique that the Company cannot,

K-VA-T
36 000039

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 157 of 199

after reasonable efforts, fill that position temporarily, then the
Company need not reinstate the employee at the end of such leave
period,

3, The purpose of this section is to provide leave time to employees
for adoption, pregnancy, childbirth and nursing the infant, where
applicable; therefore, if the Company learns that the employee has
utilized the period of leave to actively pursue other employment
opportunities, or if the Company learns that the employee has
worked for another employer during the period of leave, then the
Company will not reinstate the employee at the end of such leave.

4. Whenever the Company determined that the employee will not be
reinstated at the end of such leave because the employee's position
cannot be filled temporarily, or because the employee has used such
leave to pursue employment opportunities or to work for another
employer, the Company will notify the employee.

MILITARY SERVICE LEAVE OF ABSENCE

if you enter the military service, we will do our best to protect the
career which you began with us. The Uniformed Services Employment
and Reemployment Rights Act (USERRA) provides the guidelines for
reemployment of individuals who voluntarily or involuntarily leave their
employment to undertake military service. We will work to comply with
this and other similar laws that promote reemployment following an
individual’s service time.

MILITARY RESERVE OR
NATIONAL GUARD TRAINING LEAVES

if you are a member of a Military Reserve or National Guard
Unit and must serve a short period of active duty (usually not
more than two (2) weeks), you may take a military leave of
absence, without Company pay. Or you may arrange to take
your unused vacation with pay during your tour of active duty.

Please give your Store Manager four (4) weeks’ notice when
you plan to serve.

WORKER’S COMPENSATION

Your Company pays 100% of this protection for you. This is designed to
assist you if you are hurt accidentally while at work. The specific benefits
that you may be eligible for are determined by the workers compensation
laws and regulations of the state where you work.

While you are on a Worker’s Compensation leave status, your medical and
life insurance payments will be continued by the Company on the same
cost basis as if you were working.

if you are unable to return to work - with or without a reasonable
accommodation - after twenty-six (26) weeks, you will be offered a ninety
(90)-day leave of absence. All leave of absence requests must be returned
to Human Resources for consideration. If you are unable to return to work
at the end of the ninety (90)-day leave, you may request a second ninety
(90)-day leave of absence. If you are unable to return to work at the end of
the second leave of absence, or do not apply for a second leave, you will be
terminated from active payroll. After termination, any full-time associate
may continue health care coverage through the Consolidated Omnibus
Budget Reconciliation Act of 1986 or COBRA.

YOUR RESPONSIBILITIES TO FOOD CITY
YOUR PART

Along with the advantages and opportunities offered by our Company go
certain responsibilities—obligations that you will want to meet. Your most
important responsibility, of course, is to do a good job on the work which
is assigned to you by your supervisor. Your supervisor is responsible for
what you do, so it is a good idea to respect experience, listen to his/her
instructions he/she gives, and carry them out promptly and cheerfully.
Completing each assignment to the satisfaction of your supervisor is the
surest way to make progress.

In addition to following instructions, doing a good job requires you to think
for yourself—ask questions and make constructive suggestions. You will
find that your supervisor will appreciate your efforts to do an outstanding
job and improve your Company.

Doing a good job also implies certain obligations on your part, such as
maintaining good health and mental alertness, using good judgment,
being prompt and regular in your attendance, cooperating with your fellow
workers, and being loyal to your Company—its people, its customers, and
its services. You will want to keep well informed about your Company

K-VA-T
38 000041
Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37_ Filed 04/11/23. Page 159 of 199

so that you will be able to talk intelligently about it to your friends and

SUBSTANCE ABUSE

If we are to continue to fulfill our responsibility to provide reliable and safe
products and services to our customers and a safe working environment
for our associates, associates of Food City must be able to perform their
duties in a safe and efficient manner. Therefore, no associate shall work
or report to work while under the influence of alcohol or illegal drugs,
nor under the influence of prescription or non-prescription medication
which would affect their ability to perform their job in a safe and efficient
manner. Under the influence means not only obvious impairment, it also
means physical evidence that a substance has been consumed on or off
Company premises. No associate shall consume, display, or have in their
possession, on Food City property, alcoholic beverages or illegal drugs at
any time during their workday, including in the workplace or in Company
vehicles, during lunch breaks, and on-call hours. To do so could jeopardize
the safety of other associates, the public, Company equipment, and the
Company’s relations with the public, and is prime cause for disciplinary
action, up to and including dismissal.

Should you be required to take any kind of prescription or non-prescription
medication which affects your job performance, you are required to report
this to your supervisor. Your supervisor will determine if it is necessary to
temporarily place you on another assignment to ensure your safety and
the safety of our associates and the public.

To protect the best interests of associates and the public, management at
Food City will take whatever measures are necessary to determine if alcohol
or illegal drugs are located on or are being used on Company property.
Measures that may be used include, but are not limited to, searches of
people and of personal property located on Company premises, which
may be conducted by law enforcement authorities or by management, as
well as drug and/or alcohol tests. The above-mentioned searches and drug
tests will not be conducted if an individual refuses to submit; however,
refusal to submit will result in immediate removal from service and may
result in termination.

Associates experiencing problems with alcohol or other drugs are urged
to voluntarily seek assistance to resolve such problems before they
require management referral or disciplinary action. A portion of the cost
of treatment may be covered by your health plan, and you may have
to assume responsibilities for the cost of a portion of the treatment.
Successful treatment will be viewed positively, However, participation
in a treatment program will not prevent normal disciplinary action for a

K-VA-T

39 000042

Case 4:22-cv-00179-WMR Document37 _ Filed 04/11/23. Page 160 of 199

violation which may have already occurred, nor will it relieve an associate
of the responsibility to perform assigned duties in a safe and efficient
manner.

BEGIN ON TIME

in order for all our jobs to work together satisfactorily, we must all be
present and on time every day. If, for any reason, you are unable to be
at your work at your regular starting time, please call your supervisor so
that schedule adjustments can be made. If you are unable to report to
work because of illness or emergency, please notify your supervisor at the
earliest possible moment.

ATTENDANCE

Of course, we want you to be at work every scheduled day. However,
we know that it may occasionally be necessary for you to be absent
from work because of illness or personal reasons. In those cases, please
notify your supervisor at least one (1) hour before the beginning of your
scheduled starting time. Advance notification is necessary so that proper
arrangements can be made to handle your work during your absence.
However, notification - while helpful - does not necessarily excuse the
absence.

PERSONAL APPEARANCE

Our Company’s professional atmosphere is maintained, in part, by the
image we present to our customers and vendors. We expect all associates
to present a neat, well-groomed appearance and a courteous disposition.
These qualities go further than any other factors in making a favorable
impression on the public and your fellow workers,

Dress should be appropriate for your position and must conform to the
established standards. You may view job specific uniforms/dress code on
Fast Track or you may ask your Manager to provide additional information
on the dress code policy for your particular position. Company-issued
uniforms are available, or you may choose to purchase Company apparel
to wear instead. You should avoid extremes in dress. Skimpy or revealing
clothing is unacceptable. Associates should dress in a business-like
manner. Jeans (unless authorized by your Manager), t-shirts, unauthorized
sweatshirts, unauthorized shorts, miniskirts (more than 3” above the knee},
form-fitting apparel, “hotpants," short tops, halter tops, backless dresses,
slouch or “hang down” pants (pants worn below the waistline), flip-flops,
sandals and hats (except as required for sun protection or in production
areas) are NOT ACCEPTABLE attire in the workplace. Furthermore, the
Company’s professional image can be tarnished because of customers’
negative reactions to excessive piercings. For that reason, we do not allow
associates to display jewelry which may be offensive to our customers. Due
to health and food safety concerns, no one working in production areas
may wear any jewelry, except a plain wedding band. Other non-production
area associates may wear jewelry in moderation. Proper head covering
or hairnet and protective clothing must be worn when required by State
Health Department regulations. The Company’s professional image can
also be adversely affected by an associate’s display of visible body tattoos.
For this reason, tattoos should be covered, whenever possible, but in no
instance may they be offensive. Good personal hygiene is also important
in terms of our customers’ favorable opinion and in terms of your respect

Associates are expected to observe our Personal Appearance Policy at all

WHAT FOOD CITY EXPECTS FROM YOU
HONESTY

Absolute honesty is accepted as the first requirement of employment. The
trust placed in everyone is based on complete confidence in that person’s
integrity. Many associates handle cash transactions, while all of us are
responsible for goods, materials, or equipment of value. The good name
you can earn for honesty is one of the greatest assets you can possess. Any
dishonest act will be considered grounds for dismissal with forfeiture of all
accumulated benefits.

Always remember—it’s important to BE HONEST but it’s doubly important
not to let questionable actions appear to others to be dishonest. Your good
name can be destroyed forever by one act of dishonesty.

ENTHUSIASM

The outstanding person in any group is the one who is enthusiastic. The
enthusiastic associate will take the interest in learning everything about his
or her Company and will take every opportunity to improve the Company's
operations and services and reduce expenses. Entering wholeheartedly
into the promotion of your store, both as a successful business enterprise
and as a group of educated people, will bring greater enjoyment in
your immediate task and will mark you as worthy of assuming greater
responsibilities,

GETTING ALONG WITH OTHER PEOPLE

Some people have the natural ability to win friends with a smile, a
handshake, or a pleasant word. Others have to work at it and keep trying

46 000049

until they have learned to get along with others. Being fair, sincere, and
cooperative is the easiest way to win friends and keep them whether they
are customers or fellow associates.

Rumors

OUR TELEPHONE PRACTICE

Our telephones are maintained for Company business. Please refrain from
using Company phones for personal calls. Repeated non-business calls are
subject to reprimand.

Ask your friends and relatives not to call during working hours unless
there is an emergency. If there is an emergency, you will be called to the
telephone. Otherwise, the calling number will be taken and you will be
notified so you may return the cail on a rest or meal period.

should do so promptly and in a business-like
manner. First impressions are very important.
When answering the store phone, greet the caller
with “Good Morning” or “Good Afternoon (your
store name).” If you must leave the phone to get
information to answer the caller’s questions, or
to call someone else to the phone be sure to tell

an in-store cali, such as in the Meat Department, you should answer, “Meat

cheerful, and helpful.

Should you ever find it necessary to put a caller on “hold” to transfer a
call or reach someone to answer, it is your responsibility to check back
and see that the call was answered, NEVER LEAVE A CALLER ON HOLD FOR
MORE THAN THIRTY (30) SECONDS WITHOUT RE-ANSWERING THE CALL
YOURSELF.

ASSOCIATE AS A CUSTOMER

Food City values your business and appreciates you as a customer. We

US as we Support you.

For obvious reasons, all of us, associates, must observe certain rules of
good business in making purchases, These are simple and easy to follow,
and serve to protect us, as associates, and the store.

Associate Purchase Policy

1. Never check out your own purchases or those of your family or close
friends.

2. All persons connected with Food City will pay the full, regular price in
effect on the day the merchandise is purchased.

3. All purchases for use or consumption within the store must be paid
for immediately. The cash receipt for the purchases must be attached
to the item or in your possession at the time of consumption.

4, All purchases to be removed from the store must be selected after
clocking out for the day. Purchases must be checked out through
the checkouts and taken out the FRONT DOOR in the same manner
as any other customer. You are not allowed to set items aside for
purchase at a later time.

5. Items, once bought, may not be stored in the store work spaces or
refrigerated areas. Items found here will be considered the property
of the store and be placed back on sale, and/or destroyed.

6. Store management has the right to inspect any packages taken out
of the store,

7. Items not from the regular sales display counter or shelf,

WASTE PREVENTION

Waste of time, materials, equipment, and utilities is costly to your Company
and to you. You can help make Food City prosperous by the amount of time
you save, as well as the quality of work which you perform. You should
make every minute count. Eliminate waste wherever possible. In the long
run, you will benefit by helping to reduce operating costs because the
aout of money which is saved by eliminating waste can be shared by
all of us.

ACCIDENTS AND INJURIES

All injuries and accidents, no matter how slight they may appear to you,
must be reported to your supervisor at once. He or she will then direct you
to the proper First Aid and medical attention. Failure to report an accident
or injury will result in disciplinary action, even dismissal.

Do your best to eliminate accident hazards, but when an accident happens,
report it immediately.

finished.

normally you are not covered by Worker’s Compensation Insurance.

Lift correctly by using these simple procedures:

Never lift beyond your strength.

Get a secure footing (feet 8 to 12 inches apart).

Bend at the KNEES and grasp the object firmly underneath.
Keep the back straight and as near UPRIGHT as possible.

Lift gradually by straightening the legs with the strong leg
muscles (keeping the back upright),

f. Avoid jerky, twisting motions.

SOLICITATION

In the interest of the efficiency, convenience, and the continued goodwill
of our customers, and for the protection of our associates, there shall
be no solicitation or distribution of literature of any kind on the store
premises by any associate during his or another associate’s working time,
or by other persons at any time. Working time is defined as all time during
which associates are required to be working.

There shall be no distribution of literature of any kind, other than consumer
information (such as menus, dietary information, etc.) prepared by the
grocery industry or the government, by any person in customer service
areas or shopping areas of the store.

Any associate who violates any provision of this rule shall be subject to
counseling and disciplinary action up to and including discharge. Your
Store Manager should be notified immediately when a violation occurs.

SHOPLIFTING

Shoplifting is a serious problem in any retail business. Losses add to the
cost of merchandise and can affect job security. We should be alert to all
suspicious customers. Shoplifting is a very sensitive problem and must be
handled by experienced associates to avoid difficulties.

If you see anyone in the store whom you suspect, don't say or do anything.
Quickly report the incident to your Store Manager, Assistant, or the person
in charge. If possible, do not let the suspect out of your sight. Under no
circumstances is an associate to confront or openly accuse a customer of
shoplifting. Only a Store Manager or person in charge has the authority to
apprehend a shoplifter.
K-VA-T
51 000054

HOLDUPS

Shoulda holdup occur, remain as calm as possible, and without hesitation,
obey all orders by the holdup person. However, during their presence,
be sure to concentrate on features, dress, scars, voice, automobile, tag
number, etc., for identification at a later time.

Food City promotes an open door policy, Open communication creates an

behavior, don't ignore the situation. Discuss it with a member of
management, Human Resources, or Security/Loss Prevention. If you feel
more comfortable, you may call the Associate Response Line at 1-866-601-
5757. This toll-free number is available twenty-four (24) hours a day and
seven (7) days a week for your convenience. You may also choose to email
your concern to arline@foodcity.com. Concerns that would require you to

Harassment Theft or Fraud
